### 6.2 Service management

#### 6.2.1 Service catalog management

##### 6.2.1.1 Overview

A service catalog allows you to apply for, change, and release resources from a unified cloud service portal and provides supporting enterprise-oriented process and quota control. Service catalogs are used to control products of specific specifications within an enterprise or organization. For example, enterprises or organizations require employees to go through an approval process before the employees activate large-specification Elastic Compute Service (ECS) instances, or enterprises or organizations want to authorize only users in a specific user group or organization to activate large-specification ECS instances. In this case, enterprises or organizations can configure an approval process, specifications, and users in a service catalog. After the service catalog is published, the employees must go through an approval process when they select a large-specification ECS instances. The users specified in the service catalog cannot select the specified specifications when they purchase the service. This way, enterprises or organizations can limit which users to use resources and perform fine-grained control on specifications. The main features of service catalogs include: Allow you to create custom service catalogs. Allow you to publish or unpublish custom service catalogs. Allow you to modify configurations of service catalogs. Allow you to delete service catalogs that are no longer needed. Allow you to view all service catalogs and search for specific service catalogs by criteria. Allow you to specify the user groups of service catalogs. Allow you to bind processes of application, configuration change, and release to service catalogs.

##### 6.2.1.2 Create a service catalog

Enterprises can use service catalogs to manage resources of specific specifications or to authorize only users in a specific user group or organization to activate large-specification resources. After an operations administrator creates and publishes a service catalog, the users specified in the service catalog cannot select the specified specifications when they create the resources, or must go through approval processes to complete the creation.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Services** > **Service Catalog**.
4. Click **Create Custom Service**. Configure the following parameters.

| Step in the wizard |   | Parameter | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Basic Information | Basic Information | Service Name | The name of the service catalog. It must be at least 3 characters in length. |
|   |   | Service Type | The product on which the service catalog imposes limits. |
|   |   | Description | Optional. Description of the service catalog. It must be at least 3 characters in length. |
|   | Service Authorization | Authorization Type | Valid values: Global: Allows all users of all organizations to access the service. Specified Organization: Allows all users in the specified organizations to access the service. Specified User: Allows specified users to access the service. |
|   |   | Authorize Organization/Autho rize User | When Authorization Type is set to Specified Organization, you need to configure the Authorize Organization parameter. When Authorization Type is set to Specified User, you need to configure the Authorize User parameter. |
|   | Process Configurations | Resource Application | Optional. The approval process for creating resources. For information about how to create a process, see Create a process. |
| Document Versi | on: 20251106 | 12 |   |

|   | er Managemen de·Operations |   |   |
|---|---|---|---|
|   |   | Change Configuration | Optional. The approval process for changing the configurations of resources. For information about how to create a process, see Create a process. |
| Field Selection |   | Fields to be Specified | Select the fields to be specified. The fields for the selected service type are displayed. The fields are organized in a tree chart. For example, if you select Elastic Network Interfaces as the service type, the Area field is a root node under the Create ENI node. Region and Zone are leaf nodes on which no lower-level nodes depend. Note Whenever a resource is created, changed, or released in the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console, information about the organizations and resource sets must be provided. Therefore, the Organization and Resource Set fields are not available. |
| Cloud Service Configurations |   | Specification Pool | Add: Create specification pools that are used to group multiple organizations and resource sets. Preview Complete Form: Obtain a preview of all the configurations items when a cloud resource is created. |
|   |   | Range | Select an organization and a resource set. Note The two parameters determine the organization and resource set to which the specifications are available. Therefore, these parameters are required when you specify the configurations of the cloud product. |
|   |   | Specifications | The parameters that are selected in the Filed Selection step. |

##### 6.2.1.3 Manage service catalogs

You can edit, publish, unpublish, and delete service catalogs. You can also grant permissions on service catalogs.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console as an operations administrator.
2. In the top navigation bar, choose **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Services** > **Service Catalog**.
4. (Optional) In the search box, enter a **Service Catalog Name** for fuzzy search, or enter a
**Service Catalog ID** for precise search.
5. (Optional) Click **Advanced Filter**. You can search for a service catalog by **Service**
**Catalog Name**, **Service Catalog ID**, **Service Type**, **Publishing Status**, **Authorize**
**Organization**, or **Authorize User**.
6. Perform relevant operations to manage the service catalog.

| Operation | Procedure | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Edit service catalogs | i. Click Edit in the Actions column corresponding to the service catalog that you want to manage. ii. Follow the instructions of the wizard to modify the configurations of the service catalog, and then click Submit. | You cannot edit a service catalog that is in the Published state. |
| Publish or unpublish a service catalog | Click Publish or Unpublish in the Actions column corresponding to the service catalog that you want to manage. In the message that appears, click OK. | After you create a service catalog, you need to publish it. You can unpublish a service catalog that is in the Published state. A service catalog takes effect only after it is published. After a service catalog takes effect, it applies when users in the configured organization create resources. A service catalog becomes invalid when it is unpublished. |

| Modify the authorization of a service catalog | i. Click Authorize in the Actions column corresponding to the service catalog that you want to manage to modify the Authorization Type. If you set Authorization Type to Specified Organization or Specified User, you must also select Authorize Organization or Authorize User. ii. After the modification is complete, click OK. | After you modify the authorization, all configurations except Service Type apply to the authorized objects specified by Authorization Type. For example, if the original authorization type configured for the service catalog is Global and you modify the authorization type to Specified Organization, all configurations except Authorization Type that are configured for the current service catalog apply to the specified organizations. |
|---|---|---|
| Delete a service catalog | Click Delete in the Actions column corresponding to the service catalog that you want to manage. In the message that appears, click OK. | You cannot delete a service catalog that is in the Published state. |

##### 6.2.1.4 FAQ

**For which products can I create service catalogs?**

You can create service catalogs for the following cloud products: Elastic Compute Service (ECS) Elastic Block Storage (EBS) ECS snapshots Dedicated Host Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) NAT Gateway IPv6 Gateway Elastic IP Address (EIP) VPN Gateway Server Load Balancer (SLB) ApsaraDB RDS for PostgreSQL ApsaraDB RDS for SQL Server ApsaraDB RDS for MySQL ApsaraDB RDS for PolarDB Message Queue High-availability Virtual IP Address (HAVIP) API Gateway Key Management Service (KMS) Express Connect AnalyticDB for MySQL V2.0 AnalyticDB for MySQL V3.0 PolarDB-X V1.0 Graph Database (GDB) ApsaraDB for MongoDB ApsaraDB for Redis Time Series Database (TSDB) DataHub ApsaraDB for Lindorm (Lindorm) Object Storage Service (OSS) Virtual Border Router (VBR)

**How many product specifications can I specify when I grant**

**permissions in a service catalog?**

Alibaba Cloud does not limit the number of product specifications that you can specify when you grant permissions in a service catalog. You can configure multiple product specification sets as needed.

**How do I specify the effective scope of a service catalog?**

The effective scope of a service catalog can be users or organizations. You can grant permissions to multiple users by user or by organization. Only authorized users can access the service catalog.

**What do I do if I was granted permissions in multiple service**

**catalogs?**

If you are granted permissions in multiple service catalogs of the same resource type, a selection window appears to allow you to select the service catalog that you use to create instances when you enable the resource type.

#### 6.2.2 Process management

##### 6.2.2.1 Overview

During a process, an operation of a user is reviewed. In the console, some operations may carry risks and therefore must be approved by a user or role that has higher privilege. Processes are implemented to facilitate refined management of operations on the platform. Processes can only be associated with the creation, configuration change, and release of resources of the following products: Elastic Compute Service (ECS) Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) NAT Gateway IPv6 Gateway Elastic IP Address (EIP) VPN Gateway Server Load Balancer (SLB) ApsaraDB RDS Message Queue (MQ) Apsara File Storage NAS Container Registry High availability virtual IP address (HAVIP) API Gateway Key Management Service (KMS) Express Connect VMware AnalyticDB for MySQL V2.0 AnalyticDB for MySQL V3.0 AnalyticDB for MySQL V2.0 PolarDB for Xscale Graph Database ApsaraDB for HBase ApsaraDB for MongoDB Polardb-O PolarDB-X 2.0 AnalyticDB for PostgreSQL KVStore for Redis Time Series Database (TSDB) MaxCompute DataHub Data Lake Analytics (DLA) Lindorm Object Storage Service (OSS) Tablestore Main features: View the information of processes, such as the name, ID, creator, creation time, and status. Search for processes by name, ID, and status. Create, modify, publish, withdraw, and delete processes.

##### 6.2.2.2 Create a process

> You can create different approval processes for different products and organizations.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Services** > **Process Management**.
4. Click **Create Process**.
i. Specify a process name. The name can be 2 to 64 characters in length. It can contain letters, digits, underscores (_), and hyphens (-). ii. Click the icon between **Process Initiator** and **Actions** to add one or multiple
**optional approvers** or **required approvers**. Click the **Approver** or **Co-approver**
node. In the panel that appears, select the users that act as the approver. Click **OK**.

> **Note:** Optional Approver: If the node has multiple approvers, the operation passes the node if one approver approves the operation.
> Required Approver: If the node has multiple approvers, the operation passes the node only if all approvers approve the operation.
> You can specify the approvers by specifying users, a role, or a user group. You can specify multiple users, but can specify only a single role or user group.

5. Click **Create**.

##### 6.2.2.3 Manage processes

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Services** > **Process Management**.
4. (Optional) You can use process names to fuzzy search processes or use a process ID to
exact search a process. You can also filter processes by status.
5. Perform the operations described in the following table.

| Operation | Procedure | Description |
|---|---|---|

| Modify a process | i. Click Edit in the Actions column corresponding to a process. ii. On the page that appears, modify the process and click Save. Note If you click Save As, a copy of the process will be created. The copy is in the To Be Released state. | You can change the name of the process and add, remove, or modify the approvers. |
|---|---|---|
| Publish a process | i. Click Publish in the Actions column corresponding to a process. ii. In the message that appears, click OK. The process enters the Released state. | Processes in the To Be Released or Withdrawn state can be published. After a process is published, it can be associated with service catalogs. |
| Withdraw a process | i. Click Withdraw in the Actions column corresponding to a process. ii. In the message that appears, click OK. The process enters the Withdrawn state. | Processes that are in the Published state and are not associated with any service catalog can be withdrawn. Before you withdraw a process, make sure that no service catalogs are associated with it. |
| Delete a process | i. Click Delete in the Actions column corresponding to a process. ii. In the message that appears, click OK. | Processes that are not associated with any service catalog can be deleted. Before you delete a process, make sure that no service catalogs are associated with it. |

### 6.3 Quota management

The Apsara Uni-manager Management Console provides quota management capabilities. The amount of resources on the cloud platform is limited. Therefore, it is important to control the amount of resources available for each organization based on the scope of its responsibilities. This ensures that each organization is allocated with the right amount of resources.

**Background information**

The platform administrator can configure the quotas for the cloud products in the resource pool. The logical inventory of the cloud platform is equivalent to the quota of the root organization in organization management. Cloud services that support quota management.

| Category | Service |
|---|---|
| Computing | Elastic Compute Service (ECS) Dedicated Host (DDH) Resource Orchestration Service (ROS) Auto Scaling Container Service for Kubernetes (ACK) Bare-metal Management Service (BMS) |
| Database | ApsaraDB RDS for MySQL ApsaraDB RDS for PolarDB ApsaraDB RDS for SQL Server ApsaraDB RDS for PostgreSQL PolarDB KVStore for Redis ApsaraDB for MongoDB PolarDB-X 1.0 AnalyticDB for PostgreSQL AnalyticDB for MySQL V3.0 AnalyticDB for MySQL V2.0 |
| Storage | Object Storage Service (OSS) Apsara File Storage NAS Tablestore |
| Middleware | Message Queue (MQ) |
| Big data | MaxCompute Machine Learning Platform for AI (PAI) Hologres |
| Networking | Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) Server Load Balancer (SLB) Elastic IP Address (EIP) High availability virtual IP address (HAVIP) Express Connect Cloud Enterprise Network (CEN) |
| Application Services | API Gateway |

#### 6.3.1 Quota parameters

The organization administrator and users of an organization can create resources within the quota of the cloud products. When the quota of a product in an organization is exhausted, resources of the product can no longer be created. You must increase the quota of the product in the organization before you can create resources again. If no quotas are set, an unlimited amount of resources can be created.

> **Note:** Quotas for resources such as disks, memory, and storage capacity are measured in **GB**, and the quantities are integers. Decimals are rounded to their nearest integer. For example, 0.8 GB is rounded up to 1 GB, and 0.2 GB is rounded down to 0 GB.

**Resource Orchestration Service (ROS)**

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Stacks(Units) | The total number of stacks that can be created. |

#### 6.3.2 Manage quotas

The amount of resources on the cloud platform is limited. Therefore, it is important to limit the amount of resources available for each organization based on the scope of its responsibilities. You can create, modify, and clear quotas.

**Background information**

Quota management in the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console involves the following concepts: physical inventory, quota, allocated resources, used resources, and remaining resources. Physical inventory: the physical inventory of a specific type of resource that is available for an organization or resource set. This type of resource can be used to create resource sets for the current organizations, or their subordinate organizations. Quota: the total amount of resources available for a target cloud service. Allocated resources: the amount of a specific type of resource that an organization allocates to its subordinate organizations. The resources each organization receives are allocated by its parent organization. Used resources: the amount of resources that have been used in an organization or a resource set, including the resources used by subordinate organizations and resource sets. Remaining resources: the amount of available resources in an organization or resource set, which is physical inventory minus used resources. Resources can be used to create instances only if the amount of required resources is less than the amount of remaining resources.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console as an operations administrator or
an organization administrator of the current organization.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Quotas** > **Quota Alerts**.
4. In the navigation tree on the **Quotas** page, choose the target organization.
5. Perform the operations described in the following table.

| Operation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| Create or modify a quota | Note When you create a quota for an organization that has a parent organization, the available quota equals the total amount of the parent organization's quota minus the quotas of the other organizations at the same level as the target organization. This rule does not apply when the target organization is a level-1 organization. When you create a quota for a product of the target organization, make sure the quota for the product has been created in the parent organization. i. On the right side of the page, click the target cloud service and configure the quotas in the panel that appears. For information about the quota parameters of each cloud service, see Quota parameters. ii. Click Save. |

| Clear a quota | Note Before you clear a quota for an organization, make sure that no subordinate organizations or resource sets have quotas. i. Click the target product in the product list. In the panel that appears, click Clear quota. ii. In the message that appears, click OK. |
|---|---|

#### 6.3.3 Quota alert management

##### 6.3.3.1 Create a quota alert rule

You can create alert rules for quotas. When the usage of a quota reaches the preset threshold, an alert is generated based on relevant alert rules and an alert notification is sent. In this case, you can submit an application to increase the quota in advance.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Quotas** > **Quota Alerts**.
4. On the **Quota Alerts** page, click **Create Alert**.
5. In the **Crate Quota Alert** dialog box, configure the parameters.

| Section | Parameter | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Alert Object Information | Organization and Resource Set | The organization or resource set that contains the quota for which you want to create the alert rule. |
|   | Region | The region of the quota. |
|   | Cloud Service Category | The cloud product for which you want to create the alert rule. Note Only the products for which quotas have been configured in the organization are available. |
|   | Alert Name | The name of the quota alert rule. The name can contain letters, digits, hyphens (-), and underscores ( ). _ |

| Alert Information |   |   |
|---|---|---|
|   | Alert criteria | Used: An alert is generated when the quota used by the organization and its subordinate organizations exceeds the configured threshold. Remaining Available Quota: An alert is generated when the remaining available quota of the organization and its subordinate organizations falls below the configured threshold. |
|   | Alert Threshold | Resource Type: The value is set to the cloud product specified in the Cloud Service Category parameter. Remaining Available Quota / Quota or Used / Quota: The value is set to the remaining available quota amount or the remaining used amount. Threshold: You can specify a percentage value or a number. Alert Threshold: The threshold for the alert rule. When the used amount reaches the threshold or the remaining available amount falls below the threshold, alerts are generated. |
|   | Alert Callback | Optional. Quota Center sends an HTTP POST request that contains the alert information to the specified URL. |

##### 6.3.3.2 Manage quota alerts

You can modify or delete existing quota alert rules. Generated alerts are recorded, so that operations personnel can analyze the alert history to learn about the resource usage. This way, they can optimize the quota allocation and quota alert rules.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Quotas** > **Quota Alerts**.
4. (Optional) On the **Quota Alerts** page, click **Advanced Filter** and enter the name of the
alert that you want to modify. You can also filter alerts by specifying the **Organization and**
**Resource Set**, **Region**, and **Cloud Service Category** conditions.
5. Perform the operations described in the following table.

| Operation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| View a quota alert rule | Click the name of an alert rule to view its configuration information. |

| Modify a quota alert rule | i. Click Edit in the Actions column corresponding to an alert rule. ii. In the Edit Quota Alert dialog box, modify the settings of the Alert Name, Alert criteria, Alert Threshold, and Alert Callback parameters. iii. Click OK. |
|---|---|
| View alert history | i. Click View alert history in the Actions column corresponding to an alert rule. ii. On the Quota alert history page, view the alert history. Note The quota alert history is updated once a day. |
| Delete a quota alert rule | i. Click Delete in the Actions column corresponding to a quota alert rule. ii. In the message that appears, click OK. |

4. Select the records in the list and click **Export Selected Reports** in the lower part of the
page. The report will be downloaded to your computer as an .XLSX file.

> **Note:** You can customize the fields in the report.

To export information about multiple resource types, click **Create Report Export Task**. In the dialog box that appears, configure the parameters and create the export task. After the task is executed, choose **Statistical Analysis** > **Download Center** in the left-side navigation pane and download the report on the page that appears.

#### 6.4.2 Report pushing

The Apsara Uni-manager Management Console can automatically send reports to your email address based on the time points, schedules, and desired content configured in report pushing rules. This helps you keep track of the resource usage and facilitates monitoring and alerting.

**Background information**

A user can configure up to ten report pushing rules for each of their roles. Based on the rules, the system generates reports about enterprise operations and cloud resource usage at the scheduled time and sends them to the email address associated with the user. Only statistics that are within the permissions of the role are included in the reports.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**. In the left-side navigation pane, choose
**Statistical Analysis** > **Report Push**.
3. On the Report Pushing page, click **Create Rule** and configure the following parameters.

| Step | Paramete r | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Basic Settings | Rule Name | The name of the report push rule. The name can be 2 to 64 characters in length and can contain letters, digits, hyphens (-), and underscores ( ). _ |
|   | Rule Descripti on | The description of the report push rule. |
|   | Report Name | The name of the generated reports, which will be attached in the emails sent to your email address. |
|   | Push At | The interval at which reports are generated. Valid values: Every Day and Every Week. |

|   | Schedule d Time | The point of time at which reports are generated. Note When Push At is set to Every Week, you also need to specify the days of the week on which reports are generated in the Days parameter. |
|---|---|---|
| Content Settings |   | Specify the content of the reports. Enterprise statistics: includes the numbers of and changes in users, roles, organizations, and resource sets. Operational statistics: includes the usage and alerting of quotas. Application statistics: the changes in the numbers of resources and alerts. Note The reports will only include statistics of the resources that are available to the current role. |
| Push Preview |   | A demo report generated based on the configurations for preview. |

4. Preview the report and click **Create**. The system will send the reports based on the
specified schedule to your email address.
5. You can manage the created rules on the Report Pushing page.
Click **Details** in the Actions column corresponding to a rule. On the page that appears, you can view the basic information and push history of the rule. You can also modify the content of the rule. Click **Modify Attributes** in the Actions column corresponding to a rule. In the dialog box that appears, you can modify the name, description, report name, pushing schedules and time points specified in the rule. Content of the reports cannot be modified here. It can be modified only on the rule details page. Click **Enable** or **Disable** in the Actions column corresponding to a rule. The system will not generate reports based on a rule if it has been disabled.

> **Note:** However, disabled rules still count toward the ten-rule limit.

Move the pointer over the More icon in the Actions column corresponding to a rule and click **Push History**. In the panel that appears, you can view the push tasks that have been created, including their creation time, status, and related exceptions. You can also click Download to download the reports. Move the pointer over the More icon in the Actions column corresponding to a rule and click **Delete**. You can delete the pushing rules that are no longer needed.

#### 6.4.3 Download Center

In Download Center, you can download existing resource reports and quota reports. You can also create download tasks to export specific reports.

**Background information**

Exported files are in the **.xlsx** format. You can find the downloaded files from the downloading path of the browser. The reports you can download include **metering reports**, **resource reports**, **quota**
**reports**, **user reports**, and **detailed billing reports**.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Statistical Analysis** > **Download Center**.
4. (Optional) Filter the reports by specifying the **Report Name**, **Report Type**, **Cloud**
**Service**, **Status**, and **Start and End Time** conditions.
5. Perform the operations described in the following table.

| Operation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| Download a report | Click Download Report in the Download operation column corresponding to a report. Note You can download reports in the Completed state. If the report is in the Failed or Canceled state, you can click Retry to retry the task. |
| Delete a report | Delete a single report. a. Click Delete in the Download operation column corresponding to a report. b. In the Delete Task message, click OK. Delete multiple reports at a time. a. Select the target reports. b. Click Delete below the list. |
|   | i. Click Create Download Task. |

|   | ii. In the dialog box that appears, configure the parameters. Report Name: The name of the report. Report Type: Valid values include Metering Report, Resource Report, Quota Report, User Report, and Detailed Billing Report. |
|---|---|
| Create a download task | Note When User Report is selected, the parameters below Report Type are not available or required. The user report contains information about all system users of the platform. Service: The target product. Note You can click Select All to select all the listed products. Start and End Time: The time range of the report. Note This parameter is available only when Report Type is set to Metering Report or Detailed Billing Report. Organization and Resource Set: The target organizations and resource sets. Note If you want to select all organizations and resource sets, select the root organization. Region: The target region. Account Period: The target accounting period. Note This parameter is available only when Report Type is set to Detailed Billing Report. iii. Click OK. |
| Document Version: 202511 | 06 14 |

#### 6.4.4 Carbon emissions report

The Apsara Uni-manager Management Console provides carbon emissions reports to help you improve resource efficiency of the servers in your data centers and minimize environmental impact while ensuring the security and stability of your cloud platform. On the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console, you can view the amounts and trends of carbon emissions. You can also filter the statistics by cloud services and instances

> **Note:** Only users who are assigned the role of operations administrator, organization administrator, or resource set administrator can view the carbon emissions reports. Custom roles cannot be granted permissions on carbon emissions reports. An operations administrator has carbon emissions statistics permissions on all organizations and resource sets; an organization administrator has permissions on their organization and subordinate organizations; a resource set administrator has permissions on their resource sets.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**. In the left-side navigation pane, choose
**Statistical Analysis** > **Carbon Emissions Report**. On the page that appears, you can
view the carbon emissions reports. On the **Carbon Emissions Overview** tab, you can have an overview of the carbon emissions of the cloud platform, including the total emissions, emissions over the past 30 days, trends of emissions, and rankings of the services and instances by emissions amounts. On the **Cloud Product Statistics** tab, you can filter statistics about carbon emissions by time periods, organizations, resource sets, regions, and cloud services. On the **Instance Statistics** tab, you can filter statistics about carbon emissions by time periods, organizations, resource sets, regions, cloud services, and instance IDs. Then, you can click **Export Data** to download the statistics to your computer as an .XLSX file.

**resources**

The Apsara Uni-manager Management Console shows the number of resource instances that run in the Apsara Stack environment by time, organization, resource set, and region. You can also export statistical reports from the console.

**Background information**

You can view the usage statistics of the following cloud products or cloud resources:

| Database | ApsaraDB RDS ApsaraDB RDS for MySQL PolarDB ApsaraDB RDS for PostgreSQL ApsaraDB RDS for SQL Server PolarDB AnalyticDB for PostgreSQL ApsaraDB for Redis ApsaraDB for MongoDB Data Transmission Service (DTS) Data Management (DMS) PolarDB-X 2.0 PolarDB-X 1.0 Graph Database (GDB) ApsaraDB for OceanBase |

**Procedure**

The following procedure uses ECS as an example to describe how to view usage statistics. You can view the usage statistics of other cloud resources in a similar manner.
1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**. In the left-side navigation pane, click
**Metering Management**.
3. Select **Elastic Compute Service** from the **Cloud Service** drop-down list, and then click
**Search**.
You can configure the following options to filter the statistics: **Organization**, **Resource**
**Set**, **Region**, **Time**, **Instance ID**. You can also click the
icon to export the displayed statistics to your computer as an XLS file.

> **Note:** In the console, you can view or export up to 1,000 statistical records to an XLS file. If you want more statistical data, you can call the MeteringQuery operation.

### 6.6 Bills

#### 6.6.1 View cloud service bills

You can view bills by cloud service type.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console as an operations administrator.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Billings** > **Cloud Service Bills**.
4. View the bill overview.
i. Click the **Bill overview** tab. ii. Select the year and the month for which you want to view bills from the **Account period** date picker. You can view different data in the following sections:
**Cloud products bill summary**: View the number of cloud services that has billing
enabled, the number of cloud services that incur charges, the total fees generated within the selected month, and the total fees generated within the selected year.
**Consumption trends of cloud products.Last six months** : View the consumption
trend of each cloud service within the last six months. Click **Top 5** or **Top 10** in the upper-right corner to show the top five or top ten cloud services in terms of consumption within each month. Click the or icon to show the consumption trends of cloud services by using a column chart or a line chart.
**Top 10Consumption distribution of cloud products** : View the generated fees and
their proportions of the top ten cloud services within the selected month.
**Consumption details of all cloud products** : View the fees of all cloud services
within the selected month. Select Sort by fee from high to low or Sort by fee from low to high in the upper-right corner to sort cloud services by fees.
5. View bill statistics.
i. Click the **Bill statistics** tab. ii. Select the year and the month for which you want to view bills from the date picker. iii. (Optional) Select the cloud service and the region that you want to view. iv. Click **Search**. You can view the billing information that meets the search conditions. The billing information includes the billing period, cloud service, region, fees, and billing cycle. v. (Optional) Click the icon to modify fees as an operations administrator. vi. (Optional) Click **View details** in the Operation column to view bill details. vii. Save the queried data to your computer. Click **Export all bills** to save all queried data to your computer as an XLS file. Select the bills that you want to export and click **Export Selected bills** to save the selected data to your computer as an XLS file.
6. View bill details.
i. Click the **Bill details** tab. ii. Select the year and the month for which you want to view bills from the **Account period** date picker. iii. (Optional) Set Cloud products, Area, and Instance ID. iv. Click **Search**. You can view the billing information that meets the search conditions. The billing information includes **Account period**, **Cloud products**, **Tissue**, **Resource set**,
**Instance ID**, **Area**, **Billing cycle**, **Free amount**, and **Amount payable**.
v. Save the queried data to your computer. Click **Export all bills** to save all queried data to your computer as an XLS file. Select the bills that you want to export and click **Export Selected bills** to save the selected data to your computer.

#### 6.6.2 View organization and resource set bills

### bills

You can view bills by organization and resource set.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console as an operations administrator.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Billings** > **Organizations and Resource Set**
**Bills**.
4. View the bill overview.
i. Click the **Bill overview** tab. ii. Select the year and the month for which you want to view bills from the **Account period** date picker. You can view different data in the following sections:
**root(and all subordinate organizations) Bill overview** : View the number of
organizations, the number of organizations that incur charges, the total fees generated within the selected month, and the total fees generated within the selected year.
**Organizational consumption trend.Last 6 months**: View the consumption trend of
each organization within the last six months. Click **Top 5** or **Top 10** in the upper-right corner to show the top five or top ten organizations in terms of consumption within each month. Click the or icon to show the consumption trends of organizations by using a column chart or a line chart.
**TOP 10 Resource set consumption distribution**: View the generated fees and their
proportions of the top ten organizations within the selected month.
**Organization consumption details**: View the fees of all organizations within the
selected month. Select Sort by fee from high to low or Sort by fee from low to high in the upper-right corner to sort organizations by fees.
5. View bill statistics.
i. Click the **Bill statistics** tab. ii. Select the year and the month for which you want to view bills from the date picker. iii. (Optional) Select the organization and the resource set that you want to view. iv. Click **Search**. You can view the billing information that meets the search conditions. The billing information includes **Account period**, **Organization name**, **Resource set name**,
**Amount**, and **Billing cycle**.
v. (Optional) Click the icon to modify fees as an operations administrator. vi. (Optional) Click **View details** in the Operation column to view bill details. vii. Save the queried data to your computer. Click **Export all bills** to save all queried data to your computer as an XLS file. Select the bills that you want to export and click **Export Selected bills** to save the selected data to your computer as an XLS file.
6. View bill details.
i. Click the **Bill details** tab. ii. Select the year and the month for which you want to view bills from the **Account period** date picker. iii. (Optional) Set **Organization and resource set**, **Cloud products**, **Area**, and Instance ID. iv. Click **Search**. You can view the billing information that meets the search conditions. The billing information includes **Account period**, **Cloud products**, **Tissue**, **Resource set**,
**Instance ID**, **Area**, **Billing cycle**, **Free amount**, and **Amount payable**.
v. Save the queried data to your computer. Click **Export all bills** to save all queried data to your computer as an XLS file. Select the bills that you want to export and click **Export Selected bills** to save the selected data to your computer as an XLS file.

### 6.7 Tag management

You can use tags to classify and manage resources from different dimensions.

#### 6.7.1 Query tags

Each tag consists of a tag key and a tag value. On the **Tag management** page, you can view all tag keys and values, as well as the information about the resources to which the tags are added.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console as an operations administrator.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, click **Tags**.
4. In the left-side navigation pane of the **Tag management** page, click the name of the tag
whose values that you want to view. You can also view the number of cloud services to which the tag is added.
5. (Optional) In the left-side tag key list, click . In the
**Filter Conditions** dialog box,
configure the following filters based on your business requirements: **The organization of**
**the resource**, **Resource set**, **The region of the resource**, and **Keyword**. You must
configure **The organization of the resource** .

#### 6.7.2 Create a tag

This topic describes how to create a tag and add the tag to resources.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console as an operations administrator.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, click **Tags**.
4. On the **Tag management** page, click **Create tags**.
i. In the **Create tags and bind resources** dialog box, select an existing tag key and tag value or enter a new tag key or tag value. Click **Next**.

> **Note:** You can create up to 20 tags and add the tags to resources at a time. A tag key must be unique.

ii. In the **Create tags and bind resources** dialog box, select the organization and resource set to which the resource belongs, select the region where the resource resides, select the cloud service to which the resource belongs, and then click **Next**.

> **Note:** You can select up to 50 cloud resources at a time.

iii. Confirm the information about the tag and resource, and click **OK**.

### 6.8 Cloud resource optimization

#### 6.8.1 Resource utilization rates

**Calculation based on all resources**

**By instance**

Except for Object Storage Service (OSS), the utilization rate of a product is calculated by using the following formula: **Utilization rate of a product = 5% × Average utilization**

**rate + 5% × 75th percentile value + 10% × 95th percentile value + 10% × 99th**

**percentile value + 40% × 99.9th percentile value + 30% × 100th percentile value**.
The CPU, memory, or storage utilization rate of Elastic Compute Service (ECS) is calculated by using the following formula: CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of ECS = 5% × Average CPU/memory/storage utilization rate + 5% × 75th percentile value + 10% × 95th percentile value +10% × 99th percentile value + 40% × 99.9th percentile value + 30% × 100th percentile value. The CPU, memory, or storage utilization rate of ApsaraDB RDS is calculated by using the following formula: CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of ApsaraDB RDS = 5% × Average CPU/memory/storage utilization rate + 5% × 75th percentile value + 10% × 95th percentile value + 10% × 99th percentile value + 40% × 99.9th percentile value + 30% × 100th percentile value. The storage utilization rate of OSS is calculated by using the following formula: Storage utilization rate of OSS = Storage usage/Purchased storage capacity.

**By organization**

The utilization rate of an organization is calculated by using the following formula:

**Utilization rate of an organization = (Utilization rate of Instance 1 × Capacity of**

**Instance 1 +... + Utilization rate of Instance N × Capacity of Instance N)/(Capacity**

**of Instance 1 +... + Capacity of Instance N).** The utilization rate can be calculated by
week, month, or year. You can also specify a custom calculation cycle. The CPU, memory, or storage utilization rate of ECS is calculated by using the following formula: CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of ECS = (CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of Instance 1 × CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance 1 + CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of Instance 2 × CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance 2 +…+ CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of Instance N × CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance N)/(CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance 1 + CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance 2 +…+ CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance N). The CPU, memory, or storage utilization rate of ApsaraDB RDS is calculated by using the following formula: CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of ApsaraDB RDS = (CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of Instance 1 × CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance 1 + CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of Instance 2 × CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance 2 +…+ CPU/Memory/Storage utilization rate of Instance N × CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance N)/(CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance 1 + CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance 2 +…+ CPU/Memory/Storage capacity of Instance N). The storage utilization rate of OSS is calculated by using the following formula: Storage utilization rate of OSS = (Storage utilization rate of Instance 1 × Storage capacity of Instance 1 + Storage utilization rate of Instance 2 × Storage capacity of Instance 2 +…+ Storage utilization rate of Instance N × Storage capacity of Instance N)/(Storage capacity of Instance 1 + Storage capacity of Instance 2 +…+ Storage capacity of Instance N).

**Calculation based on time periods**

**By instance**

| Data provided by CloudMonitor is used to calculate and analyze the utilization rate of ECS, |   |
|---|---|
| ApsaraDB RDS, or OSS. |   |

| The utilization rate is calculated on a daily basis. | The instance-specific utilization rate of the |
|---|---|
| previous day is generated every day, and the data is stored for one month. The utilization |   |
| rates of the previous day are aggregated for each project, and the data is stored for one year. |   |
| The following data of a project is stored: daily CPU utilization rate, daily memory utilization |   |
| rate, daily disk utilization rate, and overall utilization rate. |   |

**Instance utilization rates**

Except for OSS, the daily instance utilization rate of a product is calculated by using the following formula: **Daily instance utilization rate of a product = (Average instance**

**utilization rate during peak hours of a working day) × 70% + (Average instance**

**utilization rate during off-peak hours of a working day) × 20% + Average instance**

**utilization rate during non-working hours × 10%**. The peak hours of working days are
from 9:00 to 10:00 and 14:00 to 15:00. The off-peak hours of working days are from 8:30 to 9:00, 10:00 to 12:00, and 15:00 to 17:30. The daily CPU, memory, or storage utilization rate of ECS is calculated by using the following formula: Daily CPU/memory/storage utilization rate of ECS = (Average CPU/memory/storage utilization rate during peak hours of a working day) × 70% + (Average CPU/memory/storage utilization rate during off-peak hours of a working day) × 20% + Average CPU/memory/storage utilization rate during non-working hours × 10%. The peak hours of working days are from 9:00 to 10:00 and 14:00 to 15:00. The off-peak hours of working days are from 8:30 to 9:00, 10:00 to 12:00, and 15:00 to 17:30. The daily CPU, memory, or storage utilization rate of ApsaraDB RDS is calculated by using the following formula: Daily CPU/memory/storage utilization rate of ApsaraDB RDS = (Average CPU/memory/storage utilization rate during peak hours of a working day) × 70% + (Average CPU/memory/storage utilization rate during off-peak hours of a working day) × 20% + Average CPU/memory/storage utilization rate during non-working hours × 10%. The peak hours of working days are from 9:00 to 10:00 and 14:00 to 15:00. The off-peak hours of working days are from 8:30 to 9:00, 10:00 to 12:00, and 15:00 to 17:30. Daily instance utilization rate of OSS = Storage usage/Purchased storage capacity

**Overall daily utilization rate of an instance**

| The overall daily utilization rate of an instance is calculated by using the weighted value of |   |
|---|---|
| the CPU, memory, and disk utilization rates. The following formula is used: | Overall daily |
| utilization rate of an instance = CPU utilization rate of the instance × 33% + |   |
| Memory utilization rate of the instance × 33% + Disk utilization rate of the |   |
| instance × 33% |   |

**Overall instance utilization rate per unit time**

| The overall instance utilization rate or average instance utilization rate per day during |   |
|---|---|
| specific consecutive days, such as 7 days, 14 days, or 1 month, is calculated. |   |

**Project-specific utilization rates**

| The utilization rates of the previous day are aggregated for each project, and the data is |   |   |
|---|---|---|
| stored for one year. The following data of a project is stored: daily CPU utilization rate, daily |   |   |
| memory utilization rate, daily disk utilization rate, and overall utilization rate. |   |   |

| Storage utilization rate of the organization in December)/12 |   |

The yearly overall utilization rate of an organization is calculated by using the following formula: Yearly overall utilization rate of an organization = (Overall utilization rate of the organization in January + Overall utilization rate of the organization in February +...+ Overall utilization rate of the organization in December)/12.

#### 6.8.2 Resource health scores

**Overall health scores of a project**

| An overall health score of a project is the result that is obtained after the status of idle |   |   |
|---|---|---|
| resources, compliance governance, and resource utilization rate in a project are summarized. |   |   |
| Overall health scores of a project are retained for one year. |   |   |

| Overall health score of a project in a single day = (Total number of resources in the project |   |
|---|---|
| - Number of idle resources in the project)/Total number of resources in the project × 40 + |   |
| (Total number of resources in the project - Number of non-compliant resources in the |   |
| project)/Total number of resources in the project × 30 + Overall resource utilization rate of |   |
| the project on the day × 30 |   |

Overall health score of a project in recent 30 days = Average of overall health scores of a project in a single day of recent 30 days

**Overall health scores of an organization**

An overall health score of an organization is the result that is obtained after the status of idle resources, compliance governance, and resource utilization rate in an organization are summarized. Overall health scores of an organization are retained for one year.

| Overall health score of an organization in a single day = (Total number of resources in th |   | e |
|---|---|---|
| organization - Number of idle resources in the organization)/Total number of resources in |   |   |
| the organization × 40 + (Total number of resources in the organization - Number of non- |   |   |
| compliant resources in the organization)/Total number of resources in the organization × 30 |   |   |
| + Overall resource utilization rate of the organization on the day × 30 |   |   |

Overall health score of an organization in recent 30 days = Average of overall health scores of an organization in a single day of recent 30 days

**Overall health scores of a platform**

| An overall health score of a platform is the result that is obtained after the status of idle |   |
|---|---|
| resources, compliance governance, and resource utilization rate of all organizations on a |   |
| platform are summarized. Overall health scores of a platform are retained for one year. |   |

Overall health score of a platform in a single day = (Total number of resources on the platform - Number of idle resources on the platform)/Total number of resources on the platform × 40 + (Total number of resources on the platform - Number of non-compliant resources on the platform)/Total number of resources on the platform × 30 + Overall resource utilization rate of the platform on the day × 30 Overall health score of a platform in recent 30 days = Average of overall health scores of a platform in a single day of recent 30 days

#### 6.8.3 Cloud optimization results

> The system generates cloud optimization tasks based on algorithms for optimizing resources. You can process the relevant resources and tasks. The data displayed is the analytics result of the previous day.

**Prerequisites**

Resource optimization settings are completed. For more information, see Resource optimization settings.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Cloud Resource Optimization****>****Cloud**
**Optimization Results**.
4. Click the **Resources to be Optimized**, **Idle Resources**, or **Non-compliant Resources**
tab.
5. (Optional) Click **Advanced Filter** to filter resources by criteria such as **Organization**,
**Resource Set**, **Cloud Service**, **Instance ID**, and **Parameter**.
6. The items in the list include **Instance ID/Name**, **Organization**, **Resource Set**, **Cloud**
**Service**, **Parameter**, **Details**, **Metric Data**, **Description**, and **Suggestion**. You can click
an instance ID and fix the corresponding issue based on suggestions.

#### 6.8.4 Resource optimization settings

> You can configure policies that are used to analyze idle status, optimization plans, and health status of resources. You can modify existing policies.

The following table describes the cloud services for which you can perform resource optimization settings, the type of the resources that can be optimized, and the names of the resource optimization settings.

| Object Storage Service (OSS) | Idle resource | Idle storage |
|   | Resource to be optimized | High disk utilization |
| Apsara File Storage NAS | Idle resource | No read/write traffic |
| Elastic IP Address (EIP) | Idle resource | Unbound EIP |
| Server Load Balancer (SLB) | Idle resource | No access requests |
|   | Non-compliant resources | SLB ports |
| ApsaraDB RDS | Idle resource | No new connection |
|   | Resource to be optimized | Low resource utilization High disk utilization High resource utilization |
| KVStore for Redis | Resource to be optimized | High resource utilization Low resource utilization |
| AnalyticDB for PostgreSQL | Resource to be optimized | Low resource utilization High resource utilization |
| ApsaraDB for MongoDB | Resource to be optimized | Low resource utilization High resource utilization |

| AnalyticDB for MySQL V3.0 | Resource to be optimized | Low disk utilization High disk utilization |
|---|---|---|
| PolarDB-X | Resource to be optimized | High resource utilization Low resource utilization |
| Hologres | Resource to be optimized | High resource utilization Low resource utilization |
| Message Queue (MQ) | Idle resource | Congested messages |
| API Gateway | Idle resource | No new connection No read/write traffic |

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Cloud Resource Optimization****>****Optimization**
**Settings**.
4. (Optional) Click the
icon in the search box and select **Parameter**, **Category**, or **Cloud**
**Service** from the drop-down list for filtering.
5. Click **Edit** in the **Actions** column corresponding to the configuration item and modify the
parameters of the configuration item, such as **Description**, **Time Range**, **Valid value**,
**Set Threshold**, and **Suggestions**. The following table describes the parameters of the

| Parameter |   | Description |

### 6.9 Billing management

#### 6.9.1 Billing overview

On the Billing Overview page, you can obtain a clear overview of the effective times of the billing configurations on the platform.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Billing Management** > **Billing Overview**.
4. On the **Billing Overview** page, you can view the effective time of each billing
configuration in the Gantt chart. By default, the Gantt chart is displayed in days. You can perform the following operations to obtain the desired information.
**Chang the time unit of the Gantt chart** : Click **Day** in the upper-right corner of the
chart and select **Week**, **Month**, **Quarter**, or **Year**.
**Search for the target billing configuration**: In the upper-left corner of the Billing
Overview page, enter a keyword of the billing configuration to search for it.
**View details of a billing configuration**: Click the name of a billing configuration. On
the configuration details page, view the detailed information of the configuration.

#### 6.9.2 Billing configurations

##### 6.9.2.1 Create a billing configuration

Billing configurations define the billing plans of products. You can configure the unit prices of cloud products based on the pricing policies or price agreements. The pay-as-you-go and monthly subscription billing methods are supported.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Billing Management** > **Billing Configurations**.
4. On the **Billing Configuration** page, click **Create Billing Configuration**. In the wizard,
configure the following parameters.

| Step in the wizard | Parameter | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Basic Information | Billing Configuration Name | The name of the billing configuration. The name can be 1 to 200 characters in length. Note Billing configuration names must be unique. |
|   | Effective Time | The start and end time of the validity period of the billing configuration. |
|   | Billing Method | Pay-as-you-go Monthly Subscription |
|   | Plan Priority | A number that indicates the priority of the billing configuration. Note A smaller value indicates a higher priority. The value for each billing configuration must be unique. The priority determines the order in which billing configurations are displayed. |
|   | Organization | The organization for which the billing method takes effect. |
|   | Remarks | The remarks of the billing configuration. The remarks must be 1 to 256 characters in length. |
|   | Billing Configurations | Configure the prices and discount rate for each cloud product. The discount rate must be within the range of 0 to 10. Note For each product, you can click Advanced Settings in the Actions column to configure their billable items. |
|   |   | Configure the billable items of each product. |

|   |   | Apsara Uni-manager Manageme t Console User Guide·Operation |
|---|---|---|
|   | Advanced Settings | Note Billable items are units of billing for the components of each product. The fees of a product are the sum of the fees generated by all billable items. Configure Filtering Condition Exact Match: Bills are generated for all billing data. Conditional Match: If you select AND, bills are not generated only when all the actual values match the specified values. If you select OR, bills are not generated as long as the actual values match one of the specified values. Filtering conditions: A condition consists of a field and a value. You can add multiple conditions. When the actual values of the specified fields in a billing record match the specified values, no bills are generated for the billing record. Note Configuration of the filtering conditions is available only when Configure Filtering Condition is set to Conditional Match. Billing Item Management: You can configure the existing billable items or create one or multiple billable items. If you click Create Billable Item, configure the following parameters: Billable Item Name: The name of the billable item. Billing Method: The value is set to the billing method of the billing configuration and cannot be changed. Unit Price Settings: Valid values: Quantity-based Price, Fixed Price, and Stepwise Pricing. Fixed Price: All specifications of the product are billed at the same price. You can customize the fixed price. The value must be a non-negative number that can be accurate to six decimal places. Quantity-based Price: The pricing varies based on specifications. You can upload a file from your computer to specify the price of each specification. Stepwise Pricing: The pricing varies based on tiers of quantities. You can customize the price of each tier. |
| Billing Configuration s |   |   |

|   |   | Quantity Settings: The quantity that is used in an hour or the quantity that is declared each hour. Valid values: Quantity and Fixed Quantity. You can add multiple quantity settings. Quantity Type: If you select Quantity, the metering is based on the usage of a specification parameter. If you select Fixed Quantity, the metering is based on a custom quantity number. Note When Unit Price Settings is set to Stepwise Pricing, only the Quantity option is available for Quantity Type. Quantity: When Quantity Type is set to Quantity, select a specification parameter that is used for the metering. When Quantity Type is set to Fixed Quantity, specify a number. Conversion Factor: The factor that is used to align the quantity and unit price. |
|---|---|---|
| Billing Configuration Verification | Instance Type Configuration Ratio | The proportion of specifications in the current cloud product whose prices have been set to a number larger than 0. |
|   | Price Verification | Indicates whether the configuration ratio is 100%. If the configuration ratio of a product is smaller than 100%, Rejected is displayed. Note The parameter value is for reference only. You can create the billing configuration even if the configuration ratios of some products are smaller than 100%. |

##### 6.9.2.2 Manage billing configurations

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Operations**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Billings Management** > **Billing Configurations**.
4. On the **Billing Configuration** page, perform the following operations.

| Operation | Procedure |
|---|---|

| Modify the basic information of a billing configuration | i. Click Edit in the Actions column corresponding to a billing configuration. ii. In the Basic Information section of the configuration details page, click Modify Basic Information. iii. In the dialog box that appears, modify the Billing Configuration Name, Effective Time, Plan Priority, Organization, and Remarks parameters. iv. Click Save. |
|---|---|
| Modify the pricing configurations of a billing configuration | i. Click Edit in the Actions column corresponding to a billing configuration. ii. In the pricing list of the page, click Edit in the Actions column corresponding to a cloud product. Then, change the discount rate and prices of the product. iii. Click Save in the Actions column. iv. (Optional) Click Advanced Settings in the Actions column. In the panel that appears, modify the billing items of the product. For information about the parameters about the billing items, see Create a billing configuration. Click OK. |
| Clone a billing configuration | i. Click Clone in the Actions column of a billing configuration. ii. In the wizard, set the parameters based on the existing configurations. For information about the parameters, see Create a billing configuration. Note Names of billing configurations must be unique. Therefore, you need to modify the name. The billing method is the same as that of the original billing configuration and cannot be modified. Priorities of billing configurations must be unique. Therefore, you need to modify the priority. iii. Click Save. |
| Delete a billing configuration | i. Click Delete in the Actions column corresponding to a billing configuration. You can also click the name of a billing configuration and click Delete in the upper-right corner of the billing configuration details page that appears. ii. In the message that appears, click OK. |

| Export billing configurations | i. Click the name of a billing configuration. ii. In the lower part of the billing configuration details page, select a product or All from the Cloud Service drop-down list. Then, click Export Price List. Note The exported files are in the .xlsx format. The spreadsheet contains the organization, export time, start and end time of the billing configuration, as well as the billing details of each product such as the billing method, discount rate, pricing rule, billable item description, billing fields, specifications, prices, counting type, counting field, unit and conversion factor. |
|---|---|

## 7 Security

### 7.1 Overview

The Apsara Uni-manger Management Console uses operations logs, AccessKey pairs, and multi-factor authentication (MFA) to enhance the security when users access systems and resources.

| Module | Description |
|---|---|
| Operation Logs | Access and use of resources on the console are recorded in operation logs. You can filter operation logs by specifying conditions such as the time range, organization, user name, and resource ID. You can dump logs to Object Storage Service (OSS) and download them to your computer as files. |
| AccessKey | AccessKey pairs are used by users to access cloud resources. Users can view their AccessKey pairs. Users can create AccessKey pairs. Each user can have at most two AccessKey pairs. Users can enable or disable their AccessKey pairs. At least one AccessKey pair must be enabled for each user. Users can delete their AccessKey pairs, but at least one AccessKey pairs must be retained for each user. |
| MFA | MFA is a method for identity authentication in addition to the username and password of an account. It can be used to improve security. You can check whether the MFA feature is enabled for a user. If not, you can enable it. However, you cannot disable it after it is enabled. You can bind or unbind virtual MFA devices. You can reset MFA keys. |

#### 7.2.2 Manage operation logs

You can view operation logs in which access to and use of resources on the console are recorded. You can export the logs to Object Storage Service (OSS) or download them to your computer. Then, you can perform operations such as behavior analysis, security analysis, resource change tracking, and compliance audit.

**Query events**

On the Event Query page, you can view all the operations performed on cloud resources through all methods, including the console, API operations, and developer tools.
1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console as a security administrator.
2. In the top navigation bar, choose **Security** > **Global Platform Security** > **Operation**
**Logs**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane of the page that appears, click **Event Query**.
4. You can filter the records by the organization, product type, resource type, and start and
end time. You can also search for records based on the username, resource ID, source IP address, or keyword.

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Organization | The organization of the resource on which the operation is performed. |
| Resource Set | The resource set of the resource on which the operation is performed. |
| Resource Type | The type of the resource on which the operation is performed. |
| Resource ID | The ID of the resource on which the operation is performed. |
| Event Type | The operation that is performed. Operations include creating, modifying, deleting, querying, updating, associating, disassociating, enabling, disabling, applying for, releasing, and changing the ownership of resources. |
| Status | The status of the operation. |
| Product Type | The product type of the resource on which the operation is performed. |

| Call Source | The source of the operation. |
|---|---|
| Source IP Address | The IP address of the operator. |
| User Name | The name of the account to which the operator belongs. |
| Time | The time when the operation is completed. |
| Operation Name | The name of the operation. |
| Details | Click to view details of the operation. |

**Log storage center**

You can create asynchronous export tasks to export specific operation logs to OSS buckets for permanent storage.
1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console as a security administrator.
2. In the top navigation bar, choose **Security** > **Global Platform Security** > **Operation**
**Logs**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, click **Log Storage Center**. On the page that appears,
click **Create an OSS export task** and configure the parameters.

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Task Name | The name of the export task. |
| Organization | The organization to which the exported logs belong. |
| Filter Condition Name | The filter conditions for logs. You can select a cloud product or a resource type. |
| Start and End Time | The time range in which the operations are performed. |
| User Name | The username of the operator. |
| Resource ID | The ID of the resource. |
| Source IP address | The IP address of the operator. |

| Key Words | Keywords of the target operations. |
|---|---|

4. Click **OK**. The OSS export task is created, and the system starts exporting the operation
logs to the default OSS bucket as .XLSX files. In the task list, you can view the status of the task. In Progress: The task is being executed. Completed: The task has been completed. You can click **Download Report** in the
**Download Operation** column corresponding to the task to download the report from the
OSS bucket to your computer. Failed: The task failed. You can click **Retry** in the **Download Operation** column to try executing the task again. Expired: The task has expired.

### 7.3 AccessKey

An AccessKey pair consists of an AccessKey ID and an AccessKey secret. AccessKey pairs are used to authenticate request senders, which can improve cloud security.

**Important**

A user can have up to two AccessKey pairs, and at least one AccessKey pair must be enabled.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the upper-right corner of the homepage, click the profile picture and click **User**
**information** in the menu that appears.

| Operation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| View AccessKey pairs | In the AccessKey Pair section, view the AccessKey pairs of your account. Note The AccessKey IDs and AccessKey secrets provide full permissions on cloud resources within your Apsara Stack tenant account. Make sure that they are confidential. |
| Create an AccessKey pair | You can delete your old AccessKey pairs and create new ones to implement the rotation of AccessKey pairs. i. In the upper-right corner of the AccessKey Pair section, click Create AccessKey Pair. ii. In the message that appears, click Close. |

| Disable an AccessKey pair | Important Disabling an AccessKey pair may cause errors in the products or applications that depend on it. Proceed with caution. i. In the AccessKey Pair section, click Disable in the Actions column corresponding to the target AccessKey pair. ii. In the message that appears, click OK. |
|---|---|
| Enable an AccessKey pair | In the AccessKey Pair section, click Enable in the Actions column corresponding to a disabled AccessKey pair. |
| Delete an AccessKey pair | Important Deleting an AccessKey pair may cause errors in the products or applications that depend on it. Proceed with caution. i. In the AccessKey Pair section, click Delete in the Actions column corresponding to the target AccessKey pair. ii. In the message that appears, click OK. |

### 7.4 MFA

#### 7.4.1 Overview

Multi-factor authentication (MFA) is an authentication method. In addition to username and password authentication, MFA provides an extra layer of protection.

**Introduction to MFA**

When MFA is enabled, you must enter your username and password (first security factor) and then a variable verification code (second security factor) from an MFA device when you log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console. Two-factor authentication enhances security for your account. MFA devices use the Time-based One-time Password (TOTP) algorithm to generate timedependent 6-digit dynamic verification codes. The Apsara Uni-manager Management Console supports software-based virtual MFA devices. You can install software (such as the Alibaba Cloud app) that supports MFA on your mobile device (such as your mobile phone) to act as a virtual MFA device.

**Precautions**

The TOTP algorithm requires that the time of the system clock of the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console remain consistent with the standard time on the Internet. Otherwise, discrepancies in time can lead to inconsistent MFA verification codes and you may be unable to log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.

#### 7.4.2 Manage MFA

Multi-factor authentication (MFA) is an authentication method. In addition to username and password authentication, MFA provides an extra layer of protection. You can enable or disable the MFA feature in the console.

**Procedure**

1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the upper-right corner of the homepage, click the profile picture and click **User**
**information** in the menu that appears.

| Operation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| Bind a virtual MFA device | i. In the upper-right corner of the MFA section, click Bind Virtual MFA Device. ii. On the Bind Virtual MFA Device page, follow the instructions to bind an MFA device. After an MFA device is bound, the MFA status changes to Enabled and the Device Status changes to Bound. Note After a virtual MFA device is bound, you must enter a 6-digit MFA verification code in addition to your username and password before you can log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console. |
| Disable a virtual MFA device | i. In the upper-right corner of the MFA section, click Disable Virtual MFA Device. ii. In the message that appears, click Disable Virtual MFA Device. After you disable MFA, you need only to enter your username and password the next time you log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console. |

**Related operations**

**Forcibly enable MFA**

Administrators, including the platform administrator, operations administrator, and organization administrator, can check whether their users have MFA enabled. If MFA is disabled for the users, the administrators can forcibly enable MFA for the users.
1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Enterprise**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Users** > **Users**.
4. Click the **System Users** tab.
5. Find the user for whom you want to forcibly enable MFA. Move the pointer over the More
icon in the **Actions** column and click **MFA Settings** in the menu that appears. You can search for the user by username, organization, or role.
6. In the dialog box that appears, turn on **MFA Settings** and click **OK**.

> **Note:** MFA can be forcibly enabled for users, but cannot be forcibly disabled.
> Before MFA is enabled, the MFA status of the user is **Not Enabled**.
> After MFA is enabled, the MFA status of the user is **Enabled but Not Bound**. After MFA is forcibly enabled for a user, the user must go to the Bind Virtual MFA Device page to bind a virtual MFA device before the user can log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.

**Reset MFA**

If the MFA key of a user is lost, an administrator need to reset the MFA settings.
1. Log on to the Apsara Uni-manager Management Console.
2. In the top navigation bar, click **Enterprise**.
3. In the left-side navigation pane, choose **Users** > **Users**.
4. Click the **System Users** tab.
5. Find the user for whom you want to reset MFA and click the username.
You can search for the user by username, organization, or role.
6. In the user details panel, click **Reset password**.
